> ⚙️ **PASS NOTE 260834-5 — THE LICENSING-PRINCIPLE TOPICAL PASS. READ-AND-REPORT ONLY: NO SOURCE INGESTED, NO NUMBER MINTED, TWO DATED NOTES WRITTEN.** The question: does `DQ-24`'s five-level ordering (Scripture / Tradition / the gathered Bishops of a jurisdiction / the Bishop Ordinary / the Rector) hold across the corpus? **Answer: YES — it is consistent with everything else on record, and levels (3), (4) and (5) each have an independent current-voice antecedent. But the LIST is new, and one thing about it is unprecedented.** ⭐⭐⭐ **`Tradition` HAS NEVER BEFORE BEEN A DISCRETE RANKED ITEM IN ANY ORDERING HE HAS GIVEN.** The corpus holds exactly two prior ranked orderings in his own voice — `LS-24` (Scripture › the consensus of the universal Church › the Bishop of Rome) and `IP-40` (Prayer Book › Articles › Homilies, a different axis entirely) — **and neither uses the word.** Where he does treat *tradition* by name (`A101-VI`) he gives it a **non-ranking, explanatory office**: it *"does not replace or supersede scripture"*, it *"helps explain what our understanding of scripture is"*, and it is ***"not on the level of scripture itself."*** ⛔⛔ **`LS-23`/`LS-24` AND `DQ-24` ARE DETERMINED TO BE TWO DIFFERENT RULES ON TWO DIFFERENT AXES, NOT ONE RULE IN TWO SETTINGS — four structural differences, each independently sufficient, set out in the close-out. ⛔ NOT logged as inconsistent and NOT logged as a change of mind; a reconciliation is available to him (`DQ-24` as `LS-24` extended downwards into the particular church) and is NAMED rather than adopted, because it requires identifying *Tradition* with *the consensus*, the step he has never taken.** ⭐⭐⭐ **THE THREE LOWER LEVELS ARE NOT NEW: `BP-49` has the Bishop Ordinary AND a *"synod of bishops"* deciding a ceremonial observance — and the synod example runs in the ENDS direction (*"we are not going to observe Lent"*, *"there's nothing in and of itself damnable for that… these things are not required by Scripture"*), which is `DQ-24` levels (1) and (3) interacting exactly as `DQ-24` orders them. `IP-60` has the generic form (*"a bishop or a priest or whoever, an authority in that church, can command that he cannot command in another form"*). `IP-86` has the Rector tier operating in the first person (Friday abstinence kept *"because it is my job to set that example"*).** ⚠️ **The VOCABULARY is unprecedented even where the concepts are not: `Bishop Ordinary` · `sect or jurisdiction` · `gathered Bishops` · `established customs` all grep-verified **0** across all 118 primary sources; `synod of bishops` occurs in exactly one (`BP-49`). ⛔ A vocabulary first is not a position change.** ⛔⛔⛔ **THE AUTHORITY-RULE / BURDEN-OF-PROOF DISTINCTION IS PRESERVED INTACT — `260826-5`'s result stands, nothing here identifies the two, and `DQ-24`(a) and (b) are treated as the separate findings they were minted as. `burden of proof` re-verified **0** across all 118 primary sources.** ⭐⭐ **SECONDARY QUESTION (element/circumstance): NOT A ZERO AND NOT AN ANSWER. He has been handed the distinction twice and taken it up neither time** — at `BP-48`(b) he names it but attributes it to his opponents in a *tu quoque* (*"the Reverend Everhard and others of his sort would say, that is more circumstantial rather than worship itself"*), supplying no criterion; and — ⭐ **NEW, and not previously logged as a datum** — in `A101-2026-08-09` segment 3 the objector puts it to him by name (*"if you see it as **adiaphora**… you're not introducing anything new to the worship"*, label `C`, s2401-s2412) and **he answers the concrete case from Article XXVIII instead of engaging the framing at all.** ⛔⛔ **A NON-UPTAKE IS AN ABSENCE: not logged as rejection, not as tacit acceptance, not as evasion.** ⚠️ **`PROJECT_STATE` §984's recorded `adiaphora` 0·0 for segment 4 is CHECKED AND CORRECT — the hit is at sentence index 2402, inside segment 3; the SRT subtitle index and the sentence index are different numbering systems and were resolved against `SRC_AAI_20260809_sentences.json` directly rather than compared.** ⛔⛔ **NOTHING MOVED: `DQ-9` unmoved · Open Q 15's two readings both still live and neither chosen · `LS-25`'s three reconciliations all still live and none chosen (⚠️ reconciliation (iii) gains circumstantial support and that is recorded as an observation, NOT as a choice) · `DQ-8` consistent with `DQ-24` and NOT reported as a conflict (order of OPERATIONS vs order of AUTHORITY) · `IP-84` confirmed and not extended · no gate, no channel state, no `VP-` pair or `DELTA`, no register entry, no hash or byte offset altered.** ⛔ **NO NUMBER CONSUMED — every number in this corpus attaches to a source utterance and this pass ingested none. <!-- PURPOSE HEADER -->

**AUDIENCE:** JD and Claude. Not Rev. James, not third parties.
**CLASS:** BACKSTAGE.
**FUNCTION:** The **only** authoritative record of anything that changes: whose turn it is, what has been asked, what has been answered, which gates are open, which levers are retired, what version each document is at, and what the next free source-tag number is.
**HANDLING:** ⚠️ **This file is short by design. Keep it short.** It holds *state*, never *argument*. Reasoning, evidence and analysis live in the corpus documents; if a line here needs a paragraph of justification, the paragraph belongs in the corpus and this file gets a pointer.

<!-- END PURPOSE HEADER -->

---

## ⚠️ THE RULE THIS FILE EXISTS TO ENFORCE

**Volatile state is authoritative HERE and nowhere else.**

Other documents may *discuss* state, and much of that discussion is valuable (routing, analysis, craft notes). But when a corpus document and this file disagree about **whether something was asked, whether it was answered, whose turn it is, or what version a file is at, THIS FILE WINS** and the corpus document is stale and must be corrected.

**Why this exists.** In July 2026 the status of one question (DQ-5) was restated in **five** places across three documents, each hand-maintained. One went stale, subsequent passes read the stale copy and re-affirmed it, and **a question that had been answered on 07-10 was carried as "awaiting reply" until 07-24** — holding the project's central funnel closed for two weeks behind a gate that was already open. The same mechanism carried question-list item 20 as "unposted" through three versions after it had been asked and answered. **Neither failure was caused by having many files. Both were caused by having many copies of one fact.**

**The discipline:** at the top of every reconcile pass, **update this file first**. Corpus edits follow from it, never the reverse.

---

## 0. HANDLING POLICY — CURRENT

**⚠️ POLICY CHANGE, 260725-1: ALL DOCUMENTS ARE INTERNAL ONLY. EXTERNAL SHARING IS SUSPENDED UNTIL FURTHER NOTICE.**

|---|---|
| External sharing (to RJ, to the Discord intermediary, to any third party, including the pastor reviewer) | ⛔ **SUSPENDED** |
| Metadata, markup, annotation, purpose headers, changelogs | ✅ **PERMITTED IN EVERY FILE**, including `On_Incense_and_the_Altar.md` |
| `On_Incense_and_the_Altar.md` handling class | **INTERNAL** · relay-clean **SUSPENDED (RECOVERABLE)** |
| `Incense_Conversational_Outline.md` handling class | **INTERNAL** · relay-clean **SUSPENDED (RECOVERABLE)** *(⚠️ inferred, not explicitly directed — see note)* |
| Validator check **C7** (relay-clean firewall) | **WARN**, downgraded from ERROR |

**⚠️ THE DISCIPLINE IS DOWNGRADED, NOT DELETED.** Relay-clean is a *recoverable* state, not a retired one. C7 still runs and still reports; it no longer fails the build. The firewall rule itself — *nothing from `RJ_Incense_Analysis.md` ever enters a relay-clean document, including changelogs* — remains on the books and remains the standard those files must be returned to before any future external release. **To restore:** flip both CLASS lines back to RELAY-CLEAN, strip any backstage vocabulary C7 has accumulated in the interim, and change C7 back to `err(...)` in `validate_project.py`. Nothing about this pass makes that harder.

**⚠️ Note on `Incense_Conversational_Outline.md`.** The policy named `On_Incense_and_the_Altar.md` explicitly. The outline was *also* classed RELAY-CLEAN (shareable), to a pastor reviewer rather than to RJ, so "all documents are now internal only" reaches it too. It has been reclassed on the same terms. **Flagged because it was an inference, not a direction** — reverse it if the reviewer channel was meant to stay open.

**What has NOT changed.** The backstage/relay-clean *separation of content* still governs what is written where; only the sharing permission and the enforcement severity changed. `RJ_Incense_Analysis.md` remains the most sensitive file in the corpus regardless of who may see it.

**⚠️ BLOG-BATCH SOURCE TEXTS ARE HELD OUTSIDE THE REPO (added 260729-1).** The `BLOG` sources — Rev. James's blogspot archive and the academia.edu paper linked from it — are **third-party copyrighted material by a living author**, and this repo is public and clonable without credentials. Committing full copies would be **redistribution**, which is a different act from fetching them to read. ⛔ **No BLOG source text is written into the repo, in `src/` or anywhere else.** JD archives them **locally, outside the repo**; only **URL, title, publication date, byline and SHA-256** are registered, in `SRC_Manifest.md`'s **Written Sources (BLOG batch)** block. **This is not a suspension of any existing policy.** It extends the handling class `Project_Bootstrap_Prompt.md` already uses for raw transcripts — stable local folder, unmodified, hash-and-scope registered here. ⚠️ **The `src/SRC_Discord_*.md` files that ARE in the repo are a different class of thing:** they are JD's own conversation logs, in which he is a party.

⚠️ **ONE COST OF THIS, RECORDED RATHER THAN GLOSSED.** With no local file in the repo, a BLOG term scan **cannot be grepped from a reconcile pass** and rests on careful full reading instead. ⛔ **A BLOG absence is therefore weaker evidence than an `LS` absence, which was grep-verified. Do not cite the two as equivalent.**

## 2. GATES

### ⚠️⚠️ THE STANDING OBSTACLE — DQ-9, AND IT IS UNDERNEATH EVERYTHING

> **Read this before planning any incense move. It is not a gate and it is not a question in a queue; it is a structural problem in the project's whole approach, and it has no owner yet.**
>
> **What he said (DQ-9, 2026-07-11).** *"The RPW is about principles and not individual acts."* His complaint is against those who use the RPW "for individual practices, demanding those EXACT practices be found in Scripture, rather than principles that are drawn from the witness of Scripture." His stated principle: *"that imagery and symbolism are to reflect the Biblical witness."*
>
> **Why it is the obstacle.** DQ-4's three warrant sources — command, approved example, necessary inference — are all **act-level**. DQ-9 substitutes **principle-level** warrant without formally retracting DQ-4. **Under DQ-9 incense passes trivially**, and so does most of the Western ceremonial tradition.
>
> **⚠️ THE SCOPE OF THE PROBLEM, STATED PLAINLY (260725-1 audit finding).** This is not one unanswered question among several. **Every prepared incense question presupposes act-level warrant** — question-list items 1, 1b, 1c, 1d, 1e and 21 all ask where *the act* is warranted. **`Incense_Conversational_Outline.md` presupposes it at Steps 3, 5, 5b and 6.** `RJ_Incense_Analysis.md` presupposes it throughout. **If DQ-9 is left unengaged, the funnel dissolves at the last step regardless of how well every prior step went**, because he can grant every finding and still answer "the principle is satisfied."
>
> **⚠️ What does NOT exist yet.** **No document in the corpus argues act-level warrant over principle-level warrant.** The outline has no step for it (drift report, 260725-1, item 2). The question list has no item for it. That argument is unwritten, it is JD's to write, and **it is the highest-value unwritten thing in the project.**
>
> **The material is already logged and waiting:** **DQ-10** (asked directly and in the friendliest terms for one thing the imagery principle excludes, it produced none — the single case offered is excluded by a separate rule about the object of worship, so the principle licenses and filters nothing) and **DQ-4** (the act-level sources he affirmed, which DQ-9 loosens without retracting).
>
> ⚠️ **Do not treat this as fixed by the DQ-15 answer, whatever it says.** DQ-15 asks about **genre** — whether vision, prophecy and recorded practice transfer alike. That is the transfer question. **DQ-9 is the level question, and it sits under the transfer question**: even a favourable genre answer leaves principle-level warrant intact and incense passing on it. **Sequence the level question after DQ-15 lands. Do not let a good answer on DQ-15 read as the funnel closing.**
>

THE OBSTACLE IS UNMOVED.** Across six written sources there is **zero** occurrence of *"regulative principle," "normative principle," "regulative"* or any discussion of **what licenses an act of worship**. > ⭐⭐⭐ **THE `DQ-19` REPLY CHECKED AGAINST DQ-9, 260833-1 — THE CLOSEST THING TO A STATED WARRANT-METHODOLOGY THE PROJECT HAS RECEIVED IN ANSWER TO A QUESTION PUT TO HIM. ⛔⛔ THE OBSTACLE IS NOT RESOLVED. IT IS SHARPENED, AND THE SHAPE OF THE SHARPENING MATTERS.** On 2026-08-21 (Discord RPW, `DQ-19` finding entry) he stated, in his own typed words: *"I'm looking at worship as, 'This is what we've received, and unless there is a component of worship we have that violates a principle of Scripture, we are going to maintain it.' So I would need a positive case to doubt what we have received"* — with the elaboration that he does not treat the RPW as *"a deconstruction of our worship"* and needs *"a positive case for why it is a violation of Scripture's principles of worship"*, not a showing that a practice *could* be wrong.
>
> ⭐⭐ **WHY THIS SHARPENS RATHER THAN MOVES DQ-9: PRESUMPTION-OF-CONTINUITY IS A THIRD POSITION, DISTINCT FROM BOTH ACT-LEVEL AND PRINCIPLE-LEVEL WARRANT.** DQ-4 (act-level, affirmed "Correct" — ⚠️ qualified in the same message as *"rightly applied"*) and DQ-9 (principle-level) are both **warrant rules**: they say what LICENSES an act. The `DQ-19` statement is not a warrant rule at all — it is a **burden rule**: it says WHO MUST ARGUE, and it defaults every received component to "maintained" unless a positive case of principle-violation is made against it. Under it, a received act never has to produce its warrant; the objector has to produce a violation. **That is a different kind of rule from both of the positions DQ-9's block already tracks, and the block now holds all three, all his, all in the same thread: warrant-by-act (DQ-4), warrant-by-principle (DQ-9), and presumption-of-continuity (DQ-19).** ⛔ **They are not harmonised here and no reading is chosen** — he may hold that received practice is *presumed to have* warrant (compatible-in-form with DQ-4), or the presumption may be doing the work warrant was supposed to do (the "permitted unless forbidden" structure DQ-4's own question named as the alternative). Both readings live; the tension analysis is `[Analysis]` at the `DQ-19` entry.
>
> ⭐ **What it changes operationally:** the burden question `Incense_Conversational_Outline.md` Step 10 carried as its owed half is **now on the record in his own words** (dated update at Step 10), and `QC-f`'s papacy/incense burden-asymmetry lever now has a general, thread-stated form of the incense-side burden rule to work against, not only `RC3-9`'s video form. ⛔ **What it does not change:** the act-level-over-principle-level argument is still unwritten and JD's to write, and a burden rule on the record answers the LEVEL question not at all — it sits beside it, as a third thing.

> ⭐⭐⭐ **DATED NOTE, 260833-4 — THE `A101-2026-08-23` ARTICLE XXXIV DATUM NOW EXISTS AND THIS NOTE RECORDS WHERE IT IS. ⛔⛔⛔ THE GATE IS NOT MOVED BY THIS NOTE OR BY THE PASS THAT WROTE IT, AND NOTHING BELOW CHARACTERISES IT AS MOVED.** On **2026-08-23**, teaching Article XXXIV to his own parish, he read the Article in full — third clause included, *"ordained only by man's authority, so that all things be done to edifying"* — and glossed it: **ceremonies must not be ordained against God's word, and *"as long as it is within the principles set by God himself in The Scriptures, ceremonies and traditions are allowed to differ."*** **Minted `260833-4` as `IP-84`; coordinates `[R]` s759 / `[S]` s734-s755, the gloss at `[S]` s735.** ⛔ **Read the finding, not this note: `IP-84` carries the warrant class, the `[R]` dropout across the gloss, the ear flag and the guards.**
>
> ⚠️⚠️ **WHAT THIS BLOCK NOW HOLDS, AND THE PASS DELIBERATELY DECLINES TO ADJUDICATE IT.** The block already tracks three of his positions, all his, all from the Discord thread: **warrant-by-act (`DQ-4`)**, **warrant-by-principle (`DQ-9`)**, and **presumption-of-continuity (`DQ-19`)**. `IP-84` is a **fourth datum and not a fourth position**: it is the `DQ-9` move — principle-level warrant — **stated as a general rule about ceremonies as such, while expounding the Article that governs ceremonies**, rather than asserted in a dispute about one practice. ⭐⭐ **That is new, and it is the closest the corpus has come to hearing the level question answered in his own voice as a rule.** ⛔⛔ **WHETHER IT RESOLVES THE OBSTACLE, SHARPENS IT, OR MERELY RESTATES `DQ-9` IN ANOTHER SETTING IS JD'S CALL AFTER READING `IP-84`, AND THE PASS THAT MINTED IT EXPRESSLY DECLINED TO MAKE IT.**
>
> ⚠️ **THREE FACTS THAT TRAVEL WITH THE DATUM AND MUST NOT BE DROPPED WHEN IT IS QUOTED.** **(1)** It is a **classroom exposition overheard**, not an answer to a question put to him — the `IP-50`/`IP-56`/`IP-59` guard, unchanged; `DQ-9` asks what is on the record FROM HIM. **(2)** It is under an **OPEN EAR-VERIFICATION FLAG**, and ⚠️⚠️ **the load-bearing sentence is precisely the one the room capture dropped: `[R]` s756 carries fifteen words across 29.6 seconds and corroborates only the tail clause.** **(3)** ⛔ **THE `DQ-9` DIAGNOSTIC VOCABULARY IS AN ABSOLUTE ZERO IN BOTH CAPTURES** — `regulative` 0·0, `warrant` 0·0, `adiaphora` 0·0, `good and necessary` 0·0, `things indifferent` 0·0 — **so he is not speaking in the RPW's own register here.** ⭐⭐ **The failure mode is the reverse of the usual one: the content is present and the terms are absent, and a term-only scan would have reported `DQ-9` DEAD on the session that produced its clearest Article XXXIV statement.**
>
> ⛔ **AND THE ACT-LEVEL-OVER-PRINCIPLE-LEVEL ARGUMENT REMAINS UNWRITTEN AND JD'S TO WRITE.** Nothing in this pass wrote it, and `IP-84` is a datum about his reasoning, not a document arguing against it. ⛔ **`Incense_Conversational_Outline.md` Step 2b was NOT touched, `RJ_Incense_Analysis.md` §2.3 was NOT touched, `QA-Art34` and question-list item 8a were NOT redrafted, and nothing was put to him.**

### ⭐ STANDING RULES AND PERMISSIONS

**⭐ STANDING RULE — HIS OWN EXAMPLE OUTRANKS THE PROJECT'S VERSION OF IT (recorded 260726-1).**
Whenever new source material shows Rev. James has **already used an argument in his own words**, the project's version of that argument is **recast as his, in every document where it appears.** His own example is always worth more than the same argument in the project's voice: it cannot be dismissed as an outside framework, and it converts a challenge to his position into a question about it. *(First applied 260726-1 to the Passover analogy at `Incense_Conversational_Outline.md` Step 6, which is his own worked example at DQ-8.)*
⚠️ **Recasting does not license overclaiming.** State exactly what is his and exactly what is the project's read of it; the three attribution layers are not flattened by a recast.

**⭐ STANDING PERMISSION — UPDATE WITHOUT ASKING WHERE THE RECORD IS CLEAR (recorded 260726-1).**
JD authorises document updates **where Rev. James's position is clear on the record**, without asking first. ⚠️ **Ambiguous readings still come back as questions**, and a fork that the record does not settle is never collapsed to make an edit possible. This permission covers documentation; **it does not extend to posting anything to him.**

**⚠️ POSTURE CHANGE — TIPPING HIS HAND IS NOW ACCEPTABLE (recorded 260726-1).**
JD is willing to tip his hand on **argument direction** where it moves the discussion faster. **This is deliberate, not a lapse.** It relaxes concealment of direction only: **lock-before-port still governs the order of operations** (the governing principle goes on the record before the specific case is named), and **hybrid pre-emption still never enters posted text.** See `RJ_Incense_Analysis.md` §12.5.

### ✅ TWO UNREGISTERED TRACKED FILES RESOLVED (260728-2, CL-5)

**§8 item 2 says every file in the repo is in §4, because an unregistered file is outside every guarantee the registry provides.** Two tracked files appeared **zero times** in this document. Both are now accounted for, one by registration and one by explicit exemption. ⚠️ **Silence was the defect; either resolution closes it.**

1. ⚠️ **`CLAUDE.md` — REGISTERED, and it was the load-bearing one.** It is agent instructions: it is read first by Claude Code and **can therefore direct an agent's behaviour across the whole corpus while sitting outside every check.** It is now in the table above, and it has been given its first `**Last updated:**` stamp so **C3 guards it** rather than skipping it silently. ⚠️ **It is a DERIVED working copy** — `Project_Bootstrap_Prompt.md` is the canonical, changelogged conventions document and **wins on any divergence.** ⛔ **Registering it does not make the two files agree; nobody has checked that they do.** A divergence audit between `CLAUDE.md` and `Project_Bootstrap_Prompt.md` is **owed work and was out of this pass's scope.**
2. **`.gitignore` — EXEMPT, deliberately.** Two lines (`.DS_Store`, `backups/`), git plumbing, no project content, no version, nothing downstream depends on its state. **It carries no `**Last updated:**` line and should not be given a fake one** merely to satisfy a version check that has nothing to check. **Recorded here so that a later pass finds a decision rather than an oversight.**

⚠️ **A GAP THIS EXPOSED, RECORDED NOT FIXED.** Nothing in `validate_project.py` detects an **unregistered** file: C0 walks the registry and confirms each entry resolves on disk, so it can only ever catch the reverse case. **Both of these files were found by hand.** A check that walks the tracked-file list and flags anything absent from §4 (with an exemption allowlist) would close it. **Out of scope this pass; owed work.**

Any check that globs the working directory for `SRC_Discord_*.md` will match nothing and report success. Derive from this registry; search recursively on a path miss; error on unresolvable.

**Repo status.** Private, internal-use-only git repository. A **pre-commit hook runs `validate_project.py`**, so a check that silently skips is worse than one that fails loudly: it makes every commit look verified.

They are the natural reading of "archives live under `/src/`, not flat," but JD stated the layout only for the archives. The validator's recursive fallback resolves either way and warns when a registered path had to be found elsewhere. **Correct the table if the fallback fires.**

**✅ `validate_project.py` UNSTAMPED DEFECT RESOLVED (260728-1).** It was unregistered until 260726-1 — the same defect class as the README, and worse, because **the tool that enforces the registry was itself outside the registry.** Registering it made C3 **WARN** on the missing stamp rather than skip it silently, which was the point of registering it. **That standing WARN is now cleared:** the file carries its first `**Last updated: 260728-1**` line, added in the same pass that extended C8 to guard `VP-`. **Scope note (260728-1):** the pass was authorised to extend C8 and add the stamp, **and nothing else**. ⚠️ **THREE COVERAGE GAPS WERE THEREFORE LEFT OPEN DELIBERATELY AND ARE RECORDED HERE RATHER THAN FIXED QUIETLY.**

1. ✅ **CLOSED 260728-2 (CL-3). C2's prefix list now reads `DQ`, `IP`, `RV`, `LS`.** The hand-check is now machine-confirmed: C2 reports **`LS-1..22` unbroken, no duplicates**, and C2's coverage rose from three series to four. ⚠️ **The closed batches (`GV`, `BP`, `RC`, `EXT`, `Rev`) were considered and DELIBERATELY DECLINED, which is the part worth recording:** none of them uses the `**PREFIX-N.**` ledger format at all, so adding them would fire **five "no ledger-format entries found" warnings on every run** — noise that teaches people to skim the validator, the same cry-wolf reasoning that removed the tag-collision detector. **The rationale is in a comment at C2 so the next pass does not re-litigate it.**
2. ✅ **CLOSED 260728-2 (approved by JD after the cleanup queue was drafted). C10 now sees `LS` in BOTH arms** — the common-ground sweep and the per-series §15 lag check. It reports **§15 is within 1 finding of the LS ledger head (LS-22)**, which **machine-confirms the hand-run §15 balance from 260728-1** (§15 grew by five: `LS-4`, `LS-5`, `LS-3`, `LS-7`, `LS-21`). ⚠️ **Both arms were checked for cry-wolf risk before shipping:** the sweep gains no owed tags and the lag is 1, so no spurious warning was introduced. **Original defect, retained for the trail:** C10's prefix list did not include `LS`, so neither the common-ground sweep nor the §15 lag check saw the LS batch, and the balance was a hand-check that would not have persisted.
3. ✅ **CLOSED 260728-2 (CL-4). C12 now scopes its session count to the sessions tables and excludes the `### STANDALONE RECORDINGS` subsection.** The headline fell from **17 captures / 12 sessions** to **13 / 8**, which is the correct figure, and the four standalone rows are **parsed and reported on their own line** rather than dropped — **a row that disappears from a validator is the C1/C6 shape, and the fix must not reintroduce it in the other direction.** The substantive C12 assertions are unchanged, as predicted. **Original defect, retained for the trail:** C12 parses every row under `# Sessions Ingested` carrying an `[R]`/`[S]`/`[?]` code — and **`### STANDALONE RECORDINGS` is a subsection of that header**, so its rows are counted. The reported figure moved from **14 captures / 9 sessions** to **17 / 12** purely because three standalone rows were added. ⛔ **That contradicts the manifest's own rule that a standalone recording gets no session row.** **The behaviour predates this pass** (the Everhard row was already being counted); this pass widened it by three. ⚠️ **No substantive C12 assertion is affected** — none of the new rows carries a role, a `SECONDARY — SWEEP PENDING` marker, or an awaiting-completion state, so the dual-capture checks are unaffected and still meaningful. **It is the headline count that is wrong, and a wrong count in a check whose whole purpose is "sessions, not hashes" is worth fixing.**

**All three belonged to one small tooling pass.** ⚠️ **They were not fixed at 260728-1 because that prompt authorised C8 and the stamp and explicitly nothing else, and scope discipline in this project is itself a rule.** ⛔ **The accompanying warning — do not let that reasoning become a habit of deferral — was heeded: ✅ ALL THREE WERE CLOSED AT 260728-2, one pass later.** Items 1 and 3 were in the cleanup queue; **item 2 was added mid-pass on JD's explicit approval**, which is recorded as such because widening scope mid-pass is otherwise how a cleanup pass becomes an unreviewed rewrite. **No coverage gap from the 260728-1 tooling scope note remains open.**

This is not a defect to fix and it is not an inconsistency to resolve:** the legend describes **what `BP` historically CONTAINS**, rule 7 describes **where such material WOULD GO TODAY**, and the two only look contradictory if the legend is read as a live routing rule. ⛔ **Do not "harmonise" them, do not re-scope the legend row, and do not re-route a closed batch.** ⚠️ **Recorded here so that a future pass reading the legend as a routing instruction has the disagreement, and its resolution, already in front of it.**

**Rules:**
1. Numbering is **cumulative and unbroken** within each prefix. Never reuse, never renumber a live tag.
2. ⚠️ **Amendment entries take the PARENT's number with a letter suffix** — an entry that corrects, verifies or discharges a flag on `DQ-7` is **`DQ-7a`**, not the next sequential number. *(Added 260724-3 after an amendment to DQ-7 was mis-numbered DQ-11a by position; corrected.)*
3. Only genuine **sources** get tags. External reviewers, critiques and commentary are logged `[Analysis]` with no tag.
4. `DQ` now spans **four threads**; the thread is identified in the finding body and in `SRC_Manifest.md`, not by the prefix.
   - ⭐ **DATED UPDATE, 260833-6: FIVE threads.** `src/SRC_Discord_Assurance.md` (*"Concerning Hyper-Reformed Tests for Assurance"*) is registered this pass and sources `DQ-21` and `DQ-22`. ⛔ **The prefix does NOT change — `DQ` spans threads by design, and `DQ-6` has been sourced from a non-RPW thread since it was minted. The rule above is confirmed, not amended.**
5. ⚠️ **`QA-*` tags are question-list labels, not source tags.** They name a sharpening *within* a question item (`QA-Euc` → item 4, `QA-Art31` → item 4a, `QA-Art25` → item 7, `QA-Art34` → item 8a). They are cited from the distinctives, so **a `QA-` tag must exist in `RJ_Final_Question_List.md` before it is cited anywhere else.** Three of the four were cited into a void from v11 to v16; check **C8** now guards this.

   ⚠️ **`VP-*` LABELS ARE THE SAME CLASS OF THING AND ARE GUARDED THE SAME WAY — BUT THEY RESOLVE AGAINST A DIFFERENT FILE (added 260728-1).** A `VP-` label is a **cross-reference label, not a source tag**: it names a vintage pair, not a finding, and it is never the citation for a position. **A `VP-` label must exist in `St_Francis_EMC_Distinctives.md` before it is cited anywhere else.**

   | Series | Source of truth | Definition marker |
   |---|---|---|
   | `QA-` | **`RJ_Final_Question_List.md`** | the tag appears in the question list |
   | `VP-` | **`St_Francis_EMC_Distinctives.md`** | a `⏳ **VINTAGE PAIR VP-N — …**` block header |

   ⚠️ **THE PLACEMENT DIFFERENCE IS THE POINT AND MUST NOT BE MISREAD.** `QA-` runs *distinctives → question list*; `VP-` is defined and resolved **inside the distinctives itself**, so mere presence of the string cannot establish that a `VP-` label is real. **C8 therefore checks `VP-` against a definition marker, not against membership** — it is a second target, not the same check with a prefix appended. **Creating a label series without a guard is what put three `QA-` tags into a void for five versions; `VP-` was guarded on the day it was created.**

6. ⚠️ **One tag, one finding.** A tag serving two findings forks its own citation trail and every downstream cross-reference becomes ambiguous. If a finding needs its own identity, it takes **the next free number**, not a letter suffix — suffixes are reserved for amendments to a parent (rule 2). *(Added 260725-1 on resolving the IP-4 collision, which sat unresolved for three months because re-tagging touches three documents.)*
7. ⚠️ **PREFIX SCOPE — what sorts is WHAT THE RECORDING IS, not how it was delivered.** *(Added 260725-4 with batch 260725-2, before the 200-plus video archive arrives and a bad scheme becomes expensive.)*

   | Prefix | Scope |
   |---|---|
   | `IP` | Anglican 101 sessions JD attends in person, 2026 onward. **Delivery-independent:** if one of these later arrives as a YouTube-derived transcript, it is still `IP` |
   | `RV` | The Revelation class series, **sessions IX onward, 2026** ⭐ *(corrected 260822-2. **Original cell retained byte-for-byte:** The Revelation class series (2026 run, sessions IX onward). *The 2025 run is the closed `Rev` batch; do not extend it* — ⛔ **There is no 2025 run.** `Rev` is **Sessions I-VIII of THIS SAME 2026 series**, ingested before session-row tracking existed. The `Rev`/`RV` split is an ingestion-history artifact, not a calendar boundary — see §5 rule 12 as rewritten.* ⛔ *`Rev` is still CLOSED and still must not be extended; that ruling is unchanged and rests on rule 1, not on the year.)* |
   | `LS` | Everything else on the channel: standalone videos, topical streams, interviews, response videos. **This is the 200-plus bucket** |

   Two supporting conventions. **A new prefix is minted only for a sustained, self-numbering teaching series**; one-off videos go to `LS` regardless of topic. And **`BP`, the Everhard response video, is a closed batch that would today be `LS` — leave it alone; do not retro-renumber a closed series.**

   ⚠️ **THE DUPLICATION HAZARD THIS CREATES, AND THE HASH CHECK IS BLIND TO IT.** Because the Anglican 101 classes are also streamed, **the same session can enter the corpus twice**: once as JD's room recording, once as a YouTube-derived transcript in a future batch. Those are two files with two hashes, and a hash check passes both. **The ingestion test is the sessions-ingested registry in `SRC_Manifest.md`, keyed on session — not the hash.** Without it, one batch out of the 200 can silently re-log IP-1 through IP-12 under fresh tags, which is the IP-4 collision arriving by a different door.

8. ⭐ **THE VINTAGE PAIR CONVENTION (`VP-`), adopted 260728-1 from patch `260727-2` §0.4.** *State only; the rationale lives in the patch and in `St_Francis_EMC_Distinctives.md`.*

   **What it is for:** the corpus now holds material from materially different periods. **A 2022 position is not a current position, and it is not superseded by silence either.** Without a convention the two get merged, and merging them is the DQ-5 defect — one fact held in two places, drifting.

   **Format:**
   ```
   ⏳ VINTAGE PAIR VP-N — <the question, stated once>
      DEEP HISTORY (<year>)  <position> · <TAG> · <source>     [OPTIONAL]
                             FIND: "<verbatim grep string>"
      THEN (<year>)  <position> · <TAG> · <file> @<offset>
                     FIND: "<verbatim grep string>"
      NOW  (<year>)  <position> · <TAG> · <source>
                     FIND: "<verbatim grep string>"
      DELTA          CHANGED | UNCHANGED | UNKNOWN — <one line, no more>
   ```

   ⭐ **THE `DEEP HISTORY` CELL, ADOPTED 260729-1 (Ruling 2, project-wide, not batch-local).** A `VP-` block **may** carry one optional cell **above `THEN`**, for material **earlier than the block's existing `THEN`**. Four points, and the fourth is the one that will be got wrong:
   1. ⚠️ **It is OPTIONAL.** A block without one is not incomplete.
   2. It exists so that a finding older than the current `THEN` can be recorded **without overwriting or renumbering the `THEN` cell**, which rule 1 and the never-alter rule both forbid.
   3. **It carries the same `FIND:` obligation as every other cell** (rule 2) wherever the source is greppable. ⚠️ **Where the source is held outside the repo (the `BLOG` class, §0), say so in the cell rather than supplying a string that cannot be run.**
   4. ⛔ **`DELTA` STILL COMPARES `THEN` AND `NOW`, AND ONLY THOSE TWO.** A `DEEP HISTORY` cell **does not change what `DELTA` measures and never upgrades or implies anything about `DELTA` on its own.** ⚠️ **A third dated point in an older register makes rule 3's reasons stronger, not weaker:** more vintage is more distance, not more confirmation. **An unasked question is still not an unchanged position.**

   **Three rules, and they are the whole convention:**
   1. ⚠️ **A VINTAGE PAIR LIVES IN EXACTLY ONE PLACE** — in `St_Francis_EMC_Distinctives.md`, **beside the newer finding.** Other documents point at it and never restate it. **This is the DQ-5 rule applied to vintage.**
   2. ⚠️ **`FIND:` IS MANDATORY ON BOTH SIDES AND MUST BE A STRING THAT ACTUALLY GREPS.** Byte offsets rot when a capture is replaced; a verbatim string survives re-transcription and re-capture. Four to ten words, distinctive, **ASR quirks preserved exactly as transcribed.** ⛔ **Never supply a `FIND:` from memory — run the grep.**
   3. ⚠️ **`DELTA: UNKNOWN` IS LEGITIMATE AND COMMON AND MUST NOT BE GUESSED INTO CHANGED OR UNCHANGED. An unasked question is not an unchanged position.** ⛔ **A `DELTA: CHANGED` may never be put to him as a change until both sides are verified** — *"you used to say X"* is the most relationship-costly sentence available in this project and must never rest on an inference.

   ⚠️ **A `NOW` cell is FILLED or GENUINELY ABSENT. An empty cell is neither and is not acceptable output.** *"Not filled"* and *"not on record"* are different facts and the corpus must distinguish them. **An absence is written up `[Analysis]` with NO tag** (rule 3: a corpus gap is not a source), and **it does not consume a finding number.**

9. ⭐ **THE SPLIT QUOTE-LENGTH RULE, ADOPTED 260729-1 (Ruling 3, project-wide, governs all future intake).** ⚠️ **State plainly what this is: the FIRST quote-length rule in the repo.** Before this pass **no quote-length rule existed anywhere** (confirmed by grep). **It replaces nothing and supersedes nothing.**

   | Class of text | Rule |
   |---|---|
   | **Rev. James's own words** — blog prose, papers, transcript, Discord, anything he authored | **Under 15 words per quote · one quote per source maximum** (the project's standing copyright discipline) |
   | **Public-domain 16th/17th-century formulary or Reformation-era text HE is quoting** — Cranmer's *Of Ceremonies*, the Articles, the Homilies, the BCP | **May run fuller.** These are **not his copyrighted prose**, and a tight cap on them destroys real evidence |

   ⚠️ **The split is about copyright, not about importance.** A long Cranmer quotation is cheap to reproduce and is the actual evidence in a ceremonial-authority finding; a long quotation of *his* prose is both a copyright problem and, in this project, an attribution risk. ⛔ **Quoting a formulary at length never licenses quoting his surrounding argument at length — attribute the two separately and keep the layers visible.**

   ⭐⭐ **AMENDED 260729-2 — THE PER-SOURCE COUNT IS REPLACED BY A PROPORTION. THIS IS A CORRECTION OF A RULE ADOPTED ONE PASS EARLIER, NOT A LOOSENING.** ⚠️ **State the reason plainly: rule 9 as written capped quotation at *one quote per source*, and the very pass that adopted it (`260729-1`, BLOG batch 1) produced a ledger that quotes roughly per FINDING.** The rule and its own precedent disagreed from the day the rule was written, and the BLOG batch 2 intake thread declared the tension rather than glossing it (patch §5A). ⛔ **The error is in the rule, not in the practice.** The amended rule, which governs all future intake:

   1. **Any single quotation of his own prose: UNDER 15 WORDS.** Unchanged, and hard.
   2. ⭐ **Per source, cumulative quotation of his own prose stays a SMALL FRACTION of that source** — as a working figure, **under roughly a tenth of its body by word count** — **and must never reproduce the source's structure or let the corpus substitute for reading the original.** ⚠️ **This is the real constraint, and it is PROPORTIONAL, not a count.** A count is the wrong instrument: one quotation from a 400-word post displaces more than four from a 17,000-word one.
   3. **Within (1) and (2), quote only what is needed to anchor a finding.** **Paraphrase by default; prefer `FIND:` locators to additional quotation.**
   4. **Public-domain formulary, liturgical or patristic text that HE quotes** (Prayer Book, Articles, Homilies, Cranmer, the Fathers) **may run fuller**, is **attributed to its own source and never to him**, and ⛔ **does not count toward (2).**
   5. ⚠️ **`FIND:` locators under five words are VERIFICATION ANCHORS, not quotations**, and do not count toward anything.

   ⛔ **What this does NOT do.** It does not relax (1), it does not license reproducing a post, and it does not apply retroactively as a permission — **it legitimises the practice both batches already followed, which is the point of correcting the rule rather than the ledgers.** ⚠️ **Batch 1's committed findings are not re-trimmed** (never-alter), and batch 2's were checked against (2) at its heaviest source rather than trimmed by count.

10. ⛔⛔ **PODCAST EPISODE NUMBERS ARE ALWAYS WRITTEN "Ep. 21", "Ep. 22", "Ep. 23" — NEVER BARE, NEVER HYPHENATED (named rule, adopted 260806-1 with the `POD` registration).** All three of the first batch's episode numbers **collide with live `BP-` finding tags whose content is unrelated** (`BP-23` is the Pusey-over-Newman alignment; "Ep. 23" is confession and absolution; `BP-21`/`BP-22` are baptism findings; "Ep. 21"/"Ep. 22" are holy orders). ⛔ **This project has been bitten twice by numbering ambiguity (`IP-4`, the `BLOG-144` phantom). A bare "21" or a hyphenated "BP-21" pointed at an episode is the third instance waiting to happen — write the episode form in full, every time, in every document.**

    ⚠️ **Note for future editors, added 260811-1: this rule's NUMBER is cited as "§5 rule 10" from `St_Francis_EMC_Distinctives.md`'s Source ID Legend and from the `260806-1` pass note. The rule added this pass was therefore appended as rule 11, deliberately AFTER this one, so that rule 10 did not shift. ⛔ Do not renumber rules 1-10 — append.**

11. ⭐⭐ **A PASS NOTE MUST NOT ASSERT ITS OWN COMMIT STATE (named rule, adopted 260811-1 after a five-instance staleness sweep).**

    ⛔ **The rule.** A pass note, close-out or changelog entry **must not state whether the pass it describes is committed, uncommitted, pushed, or awaiting review.** ⭐ **Prescribed neutral wording where the status of the work needs saying at all: *"emitted for JD's review and applied at his direction."*** ⚠️ **Simply omitting the claim is equally correct and usually better** — git already records the commit state exactly, and it records it accurately.

    ⚠️⚠️ **THE REASONING, RECORDED BECAUSE THE RULE IS USELESS WITHOUT IT.** ⛔ **This is structural, not incidental, and it is not a matter of anyone having been careless.** **A close-out is written BEFORE the commit exists.** At the moment of writing, *"uncommitted; awaiting his review"* is **true** — it is the most accurate sentence available. The instant JD commits and pushes, **the same sentence becomes false, inside text that the never-alter rule forbids rewriting.** ⭐ **So the defect is not in any pass's judgement; it is in the CLASS of claim. A document cannot truthfully assert a fact that postdates it, and no amount of care at writing time can fix that.** ⛔⛔ **It will therefore recur on EVERY pass unless the wording changes — which is why the fix is a convention and not a correction.**

    ⚠️ **SAME DEFECT CLASS AS THE `DQ-15` STALENESS SWEEP (`260802-1`), AND THE COMPARISON IS INSTRUCTIVE.** That sweep cleared 15 locations across 4 documents asserting a question status that later moved. **Both are volatile state duplicated outside `PROJECT_STATE.md` — rule 5's own hazard, and what `C5` exists to measure.** ⭐ **Commit state is the purest case of it: the single most volatile fact about a pass, asserted in the one place that may never be edited.**

    ⛔ **What this rule does NOT do.** It does not alter a single existing sentence. **The five instances found at `260811-1` — four in this file's `260810-1`, `260809-1`, `260808-1` and `260802-1` pass notes, one mirrored in `St_Francis_EMC_Distinctives.md`'s `260810-1` note — all STAND AS WRITTEN**, each covered by a dated note (individually for the two most recent, and by a single combined note for the two earlier ones). ⚠️ **They were true when written. That is exactly the point of the rule and exactly why they are kept.**

    ⚠️ **NOT MACHINE-GUARDED, AND THAT IS RECORDED RATHER THAN GLOSSED.** No validator check enforces this; a `C5`-style scan for *"uncommitted"*/*"awaiting his review"* in pass notes is the obvious candidate and **was considered and NOT built this pass** (out of scope, and it would need to exempt the dated notes that explain the corrections, or it would flag its own fix). ⏳ **Logged as available tooling work; until then this is a HUMAN discipline enforced at close-out, like rule 6.**

12. ⭐⭐ **EVERY `Rev`/`RV` CITATION CARRIES ITS YEAR (named rule, adopted 260813-1 with the 2025 Revelation retro-registration).**

    ⛔⛔⛔ **THE RULE'S PREMISE WAS FALSE AND THE RULE IS REWRITTEN — CORRECTED 260822-2. READ THE REWRITTEN RULE BELOW, NOT THE ORIGINAL, WHICH IS RETAINED IMMEDIATELY AFTER IT PER THE NEVER-ALTER RULE.**

    ⭐⭐⭐ **THE REWRITTEN RULE (260822-2).** **`Rev` and `RV` are ONE CONTINUOUS SERIES, TAUGHT ENTIRELY IN 2026.** ⛔ **They are NOT two runs of the same class a year apart — that was an error of ingestion history, not a fact about the material.** Every citation of either **carries its year and its batch marker, every time, in every document**: **`Rev-9 (2026, early series)`**, **`RV-3 (2026, tracked series)`**. ⛔ **Never bare, and never *"(2025)"* in newly written text.**

    ⚠️ **WHAT THE TWO MARKERS NOW MEAN, SINCE THEY NO LONGER MEAN TWO YEARS.** ⭐ **`(2026, early series)`** = **Sessions I-VIII**, ingested as *"Batch 6"* on 2026-06-13, **before `SRC_Manifest.md` or any session-row discipline existed**. ⭐ **`(2026, tracked series)`** = **Sessions IX onward**, ingested after that discipline began, each with a registered session row, file, hash and byte range. ⛔⛔ **THE PREFIX BOUNDARY RECORDS WHEN THE PROJECT GOT ORGANISED, NOT WHEN THE CLASS WAS TAUGHT.** ⚠️ **The distinction is still worth carrying** — it is a real and material difference in **provenance strength**, and a reader needs to know whether a citation rests on a registered row or on a pre-manifest batch — **but it is a PROVENANCE marker and must never again be read, written or reasoned about as a DATE.**

    ⛔⛔ **THE NEAR-HOMOGRAPH DANGER IS UNDIMINISHED AND THE RULE STILL BINDS FOR THAT REASON.** `Rev` and `RV` still **differ by one character and its case**, still name **the same class, the same man, the same book, the same parish** — ⭐ **and now, the same series, which makes a confusion between them MORE likely to pass unnoticed, not less.** ⚠️ **What the correction removes is the *"superseded position"* hazard below, not the collision hazard: there is no longer any risk of quoting a year-old view as current, because there is no year-old view. ⛔ There remains every risk of citing the wrong session.**

    ⛔ **ORIGINAL RULE TEXT, RETAINED BYTE-FOR-BYTE BELOW — an honest and recorded belief at 260813-1, now known wrong. ⛔ It is preserved with its original markup so the record of what was believed is exact:**

    ⛔ **The rule.** **`Rev` (2025) and `RV` (2026) are two runs of the same class.** Every citation of either **carries its year, every time, in every document**: **`Rev-9 (2025)`**, **`RV-3 (2026)`**. ⛔ **Never bare.**

    ⚠️⚠️ **THE REASONING, RECORDED BECAUSE THE RULE IS USELESS WITHOUT IT — AND BECAUSE THIS IS THE WORST COLLISION SHAPE IN THE PROJECT SO FAR.** The two prefixes **differ by ONE CHARACTER AND ITS CASE.** ⛔ **`Rev` and `RV` are not merely similar; they are near-homographs naming the same class taught by the same man on the same book to the same parish, one year apart.** Every other collision this project has been bitten by was between **unrelated** things that happened to share a number — `IP-4`'s two findings, the `BLOG-144` phantom, and the **`Ep. 23` / `BP-23`** case that produced rule 10, where an episode number collided with a finding tag **whose content was unrelated.** ⭐⭐ **THIS ONE IS WORSE, AND THE REASON IS EXACTLY THAT THE CONTENT IS RELATED.** A reader who confuses `BP-23` with "Ep. 23" is looking at Pusey-over-Newman when they wanted confession and absolution, and **the mismatch announces itself immediately.** A reader who confuses `Rev-9 (2025)` with `RV-9 (2026)` is looking at **plausible, adjacent, same-subject material** — and **nothing announces the error at all.**

    ⛔⛔⛔ **THE PARAGRAPH BELOW IS RETAINED UNALTERED AND ITS CENTRAL CLAIM IS WITHDRAWN — CORRECTED 260822-2.** ⭐⭐ **THE SUPERSEDED-POSITION HAZARD DOES NOT ARISE FOR `Rev` AT ALL, BECAUSE THERE IS NO EARLIER RUN TO BE SUPERSEDED.** `Rev-1`…`Rev-11` are **Sessions I-VIII of the same 2026 series** as `RV`; they are **current voice**, exactly as `RV` is. ⛔ **A dropped year on a `Rev` citation therefore does NOT convert a superseded statement into an apparently current one — the statement WAS current.** ⚠️⚠️ **THIS MAKES THE RULE LESS URGENT AND NOT LESS BINDING, AND THE DISTINCTION MATTERS: the reason to carry the marker is now SESSION IDENTIFICATION AND PROVENANCE STRENGTH, not vintage.** ⛔ **Rule 7's ban on merging the two tag series is UNAFFECTED and still binds — see the corrected note at rule 7.** ⛔ **Original paragraph follows:**

    ⛔⛔ **AND THE FAILURE IS NOT SYMMETRICAL WITH ITS COST.** The `Rev` run is **2025**; the `RV` run is **2026**. ⛔ **The single most damaging thing this project can do is put a superseded position to Rev. James as though it were his current one** — the same hazard `VP-`'s `DELTA: CHANGED` guard exists to prevent (rule 8, rule 3: *"you used to say X"* is the most relationship-costly sentence available here). **A dropped year on a `Rev` citation converts a 2025 statement into an undated one, and an undated one reads as current.** ⚠️ **Rule 7 already forbids MERGING the two tag series. This rule closes the gap rule 7 leaves open: you can keep two series perfectly separate and still cite one of them in a form that does not say which it is.**

    ⛔ **WHAT THIS RULE DOES NOT DO — and this is the part most likely to be got wrong.** ⛔⛔ **IT IS NOT RETROACTIVE. Existing `Rev-`/`RV-` citations are NOT retro-edited.** There are dozens across the corpus and **rewriting them would be a mass edit of committed findings for a readability gain**, which the never-alter rule forbids and which rule 1's own logic (a renumbering costs more than the ambiguity it removes) argues against. ⭐ **The convention is prospective: it governs what is WRITTEN from 260813-1 onward.** ⚠️ **A bare `Rev-9` encountered in existing text is therefore NOT a defect and must not be "fixed" — it predates the rule, and the Source ID Legend row for `Rev` now carries the disambiguation for anyone who lands on one.**

    ⭐⭐⭐ **AND THE SAME NON-RETROACTIVITY NOW GOVERNS THE 2025→2026 CORRECTION ITSELF — RULED 260822-2, AND THIS IS THE GOVERNING NOTICE FOR THE WHOLE CORPUS.** ⛔⛔ **EVERY EXISTING `Rev-N (2025)` CITATION IN EVERY DOCUMENT IS TO BE READ AS `Rev-N (2026, early series)`. There are FORTY such Revelation-bearing `(2025)` occurrences in `St_Francis_EMC_Distinctives.md` and they are DELIBERATELY NOT RETRO-EDITED**, on this rule's own reasoning, which applies here with more force than it did at 260813-1: **rewriting forty committed citations inside a findings corpus is exactly the mass edit the never-alter rule forbids**, and the ambiguity it would remove is already removed by this notice. ⭐ **What IS corrected in place is every site that ASSERTS the two-run claim** — the legends, the section headings, the session table, the comparison framing — **because those instruct future writing, and a wrong instruction is a defect in a way that a wrong label beside a correct finding is not.** ⚠️⚠️ **THE DISCRIMINATION THAT MATTERS WHEN READING OR GREPPING FOR THIS: `(2025)` IS STILL CORRECT ON THE `RC`, `RC3`, `RC4`, `ANF` AND `COT` SERIES, WHICH REALLY ARE 2025 MATERIAL.** ⛔ **A pass that globally replaces `(2025)` with `(2026)` will corrupt FOURTEEN correct citations to fix forty wrong ones. The corrected set is the REVELATION-BEARING occurrences ONLY — see the 260822-2 pass note for the line-level inventory.**

    ⚠️ **NOT MACHINE-GUARDED.** No validator check enforces this. A `C8`-style scan for `\bRev-\d+\b` not followed by a year is buildable but **would fire on every pre-260813-1 citation in the corpus** — i.e. on exactly the text this rule declines to touch — so it would need a date-scoped exemption it has no way to compute. ⏳ **Logged as available tooling work; until then, human discipline at write time, like rules 6 and 11.**


---

## 6. INTAKE CHECKLIST — run every time

1. **Note the handover date.** Relative timestamps ("Yesterday", "Today") are relative to **the date JD supplies the paste**, not to the intake session.
2. **Resolve every relative timestamp to an absolute date, in the `SRC_Discord_*.md` file itself**, before logging anything.
3. **Update `PROJECT_STATE.md` FIRST** — channel state, question state, gates.
4. Log findings in the corpus, each dated from its **message timestamp**.
5. Patch the question list and topical analyses.
6. **Run `validate_project.py`.** Fix everything it reports.
7. Update version stamps and changelogs.

### ⭐ 8. ANCHOR BY HEADING TEXT, NEVER BY SECTION NUMBER (standing rule, adopted 260728-2, CL-2)

⚠️ **A patch block emitted in APPEND MODE must anchor each finding by the TARGET HEADING'S TEXT, not by its § number.**

```
Anchor: "Priesthood & Absolution"        ✅ correct
Anchor: §5 (ministry)                    ⛔ wrong, and silently so
```

**Why this is a rule and not a preference.** The `260727-2` patch had **five of eight § anchors wrong by number** (`§2 self-location` → really §1; `§5 ministry` → §11; `§7 formularies` → `§Formularies`; `§11 Rome` → §3). ⛔ **Five of eight is not a slip. It is a process with no feedback loop:** an intake thread in APPEND MODE has **never seen the distinctives**, so it emits numbers it has no way to verify, and the reconcile thread absorbs the error silently by placing findings from the anchor's *description*.

**Two properties make heading text strictly better.** It is **greppable**, so the reconcile thread can verify an anchor mechanically instead of interpreting it. And it is **stable under renumbering** — *a patch that says `Anchor: "Ministry and succession"` cannot be wrong by renumbering, whereas every § number in every open patch goes stale the moment a section is inserted.*

⚠️ **`St_Francis_EMC_Distinctives.md` MIXES NUMBERED AND NAMED SECTIONS** (`## 11. Priesthood & Absolution` alongside `## §Formularies — …`), **so a bare § number is not even well-defined for part of the document.** That alone is sufficient reason.

**Reconcile-side rule:** if a patch arrives with a § number anyway, **place by the description and record the mapping**, per disk-wins — and **correct the emitting prompt**, because an unrecorded mapping is a defect generator that survives the pass.

## 8. RECURRING CURRENCY AUDIT — the checklist

> **⛔ RETIRED 260725-1: the distinctives JSONL re-encode.** The former §8 staged a machine-readable conversion of `St_Francis_EMC_Distinctives.md`. **JD is satisfied with the prose format; the proposal is retired, not deferred.** Do not re-propose it. Its one durable warning is preserved elsewhere and still binds: *never flatten the three-layer attribution system, and never drop the do-not-deploy guards.* That warning now applies to any tooling that touches the corpus, not to a conversion that is not happening.

**Why this replaced it.** The re-encode was a solution to slow retrieval. The failures this project has actually sustained were **currency failures, not retrieval failures**: a stale status carried nine days (DQ-5), a stale item carried three versions (item 20), two validator checks running zero times while reporting clean, three question IDs cited into a void, one tag doing double duty for three months, a §15 that had not moved since the RC batch, and a README a month out of date. Every one of those is a thing that was true once and quietly stopped being true. **This checklist is the instrument for that class.**

**Cadence:** every reconcile pass, and any standalone pass tagged CURRENCY AUDIT. **Run in order.** Items 1-3 are mechanical; 4-8 need judgment and cannot be delegated to the validator.

### 1. Tooling first — did the checks actually run?
- [ ] Run `python3 validate_project.py` (add a root path if not run from the repo root).
- [ ] **Read the COVERAGE SUMMARY before reading the results.** A check reporting **0 files seen is a failure**, regardless of the error count. The run exits non-zero on zero-coverage precisely so this cannot be skimmed past.
- [ ] Confirm every registered path in §4 resolved **exactly**. A `[C0] resolved by search` warning means the registry is wrong; fix the registry, not the file.
- [ ] Remember the pre-commit hook: a silent skip makes every commit since the defect look verified. **Distrust a clean pass you did not read the coverage line of.**

### 2. Stamps and registry
- [ ] Every document's `**Last updated:**` stamp matches its §4 registry row (C3).
- [ ] Every file in the repo is *in* §4. **An unregistered file is outside every guarantee** — that is how the README drifted a month.
- [ ] Stamps stay machine-parseable. `PROJECT_STATE.md` and `SRC_Manifest.md` were versioned in **prose only** until 260725-1 and were therefore uncheckable by C3; both now carry a standard `**Last updated:**` line. **Do not revert either to a prose-only version note.**

### 3. Cross-reference integrity
- [ ] Question IDs cited in the distinctives all resolve in the question list (C8).
- [ ] No do-not-deploy item is still marked deployable in the question list (C9).
- [ ] Source tags: numbering unbroken, no duplicates, no tag doing double duty (C2). **A tag serving two findings is a citation trail that silently forks.**
- [ ] **Sessions, not hashes** (C12, added 260725-4). Every session row in `SRC_Manifest.md` marked `SECONDARY — SWEEP PENDING` has a recorded sweep; every byte offset cited after 260725 carries a capture code. **A registered-but-unreconciled second capture is the two-copies-of-one-fact condition this file exists to prevent, and it must not sit quietly across commits.**

### 4. ⚠️ THE BALANCE CHECK — is §15 keeping pace?
- [ ] Count DQ/IP findings added since the last pass. Count §15 additions since the last pass. **If tensions grew and §15 did not, the source-of-truth document is drifting adversarial** (C10 flags this mechanically; the judgment is yours).
- [ ] Sweep the pass's new findings for anything marked *common ground*, *credit it*, *formulary-faithful*, *do not deploy*, or *no lever here* — and confirm each was actually folded into §15, not merely noted in an analysis block. **Noted is not logged.** Every §15 addition made on 260725-1 had been sitting in an analysis note, some for two weeks.
- [ ] Ask directly: *would RJ recognise himself in §15?* If §15 reads thinner than the tensions sections, the corpus is no longer a record of a man; it is a brief against him.

### 5. Volatile state
- [ ] Channel state, gates, and question state in §1-§3 updated **before** any corpus edit.
- [ ] No corpus document asserts a status that contradicts this file (C4).
- [ ] Every relative timestamp in every `src/SRC_Discord_*.md` resolved to an absolute date **in the archive file itself** (C1). **This is the July 2026 bug.**
- [ ] Archive hashes match `SRC_Manifest.md` (C6).

### 6. Retired levers
- [ ] Anything answered formulary-faithfully this pass added to the §3 do-not-deploy register.
- [ ] Nothing on the register has quietly reactivated in the question list.

### 7. Derived and dependent documents
- [ ] Documents carrying a derivation pointer re-checked against their source (currently: `Incense_Conversational_Outline.md` ← `RJ_Incense_Analysis.md` + the distinctives). **Report drift; do not rewrite JD's reasoning without asking** (C11).
- [ ] Handling classes in §0 still match the CLASS line in each file's purpose header.

### 8. Close the pass
- [ ] Changelogs prepended, never altered.
- [ ] Version stamps bumped in both the document and the §4 registry.
- [ ] Re-run the validator. **Read the coverage summary again.**
- [ ] ⚠️ **WAS THIS PASS INTERRUPTED (tool-use limit, timeout, anything that split the emission)?** If so, **re-emit the ENTIRE registered set and replace the working tree wholesale.** Do not cherry-pick the files that changed. *(Added 260726-1.)* **Why:** a pass interrupted mid-close-out leaves **stamps bumped and the registry stale**, and a partial download across the interruption produces a working tree that **no single check can diagnose** — C3 reports drift but names the innocent files, because the stale file is the one doing the checking. **A partial emission is the defect; wholesale replacement is the only reliable fix.**
- [ ] ⚠️ **If a pass cannot emit the full set, say so explicitly and name every file still owed**, so the set is never silently incomplete.

**⚠️ Do not run a currency audit in the same session as a live intake.** Intake creates the state this checklist audits; auditing it in the same breath audits your own unfinished work.
